    By \eqref{eq:projected-local-HS-bound}, \eqref{eq:mbar-linear-combo} of Lemma \ref{lem:m-representation}, \eqref{eq:m-response-op-bound} of Lemma \ref{lem:m-representation}, and Lemma \ref{lem:hs-composition}, we get
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \mathfrak{Q}_{k,t,r,a}\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{HS})} \le C_m
    \end{equation}
    Let $M_{k,t,r,a}$ be the matrix representation of $\mathfrak{Q}$ as in Lemma \ref{lem:bilinear-matrix}, so that $\left\lVert M_{k,t,r,a}\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{F})} \le C_m$. Now we fix the $(k,t,r,a)$ subscript and suppress it. Write
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak{Q}(u,u) = \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr}(M) + \left(u^\top M u - \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr}(M) \right)
    \end{equation}
    We have from Corollary \ref{cor:scalar-bounds} and Proposition \ref{prop:bar-alpha}(ii), and Cauchy--Schwarz
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \beta^r_{i,0}(u) d^a(u)\right\rVert_{L^2} \,\le\, Cd^{-3/2}, \quad \left\lVert \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr}(M)\right\rVert_{L^2} \,\le\, p^{-1/2}\left\lVert M\right\rVert_{L^2(\mathrm{F})} \,\le\, C d^{-1}
    \end{equation}
    We see that the trace part contributes $\mathcal{O}(d^{-5/2})$ to $\mathfrak B_{k,t}(\bar m, s)$ and thus is negligible. But by Lemma \ref{lem:refined-centered-quad}, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert u^\top M u - \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr}(M)\right\rVert_{L^2} \le Cd^{-3/2}
    \end{equation}
    So the centered quadratic form contributes $\mathcal{O}(d^{-3})$. Therefore, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb{E} \left\lvert \mathfrak B_{k,t}(\bar m, s)\right\rvert \le C d^{-5/2}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
By \eqref{eq:mbar-linear-combo} of Lemma \ref{lem:m-representation}, with $\mathcal R_{k,r}$ defined in \eqref{eq:Rtr-def}, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \mathrm{I}^{(2,\mathrm{lin})}_t(u) \,=\, \sum_{k\,=\,0}^{t-1}\sum_{r,s\,=\,0}^k \beta^r_{i,0}(u)\beta^s_{i,0}(u) \mathfrak{Q}_{k,t,r,s}(u,u)
    \end{equation}
    with bilinear form $\mathfrak{Q}_{k,t,r,s}(u_1, u_2) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \mathfrak B_{k,t}(\mathcal R_{k,r} u_1, \mathcal R_{k,s} u_2) $. By \eqref{eq:projected-local-HS-bound}, \eqref{eq:mbar-linear-combo} of Lemma \ref{lem:m-representation}, and Lemma \ref{lem:hs-composition}, we have that $\left\lVert \mathfrak{Q}_{k,t,r,s}\right\rVert_{L^m}\le C_m$. Now let $M_{k,t,r,s}$ be the symmetric matrix representation of $\mathfrak{Q}_{k,t,r,s}$ from Lemma \ref{lem:bilinear-matrix} Then we have
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert M_{k,t,r,s}\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{F})} \le \left\lVert \mathfrak{Q}_{k,t,r,s}\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{HS})} \le C_m
    \end{equation}
    Write
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak{Q}_{k,t,r,s}(u,u) \,=\, \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr}(M_{k,t,r,s}) + \tilde m_{k,t,r,s}(u), \quad \tilde m_{k,t,r,s}(u) \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \left(u^\top M_{k,t,r,s} u - \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr}(M_{k,t,r,s}) \right)
    \end{equation}
    By Lemma \ref{lem:refined-centered-quad}, we have $\left\lVert \tilde m_{k,t,r,s}(u)\right\rVert_{L^2} \le C d^{-3/2}$. We thus write
    \begin{equation}
        \mathrm{I}^{(2,\mathrm{lin})}_t(u) = \mathsf T^\mathrm{lin}(u) + \mathsf R^\mathrm{lin}(u)
    \end{equation}
    where
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \mathsf T^\mathrm{lin}(u) &\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \sum_{k\,=\,0}^{t-1} \sum_{r,s\,=\,0}^k \frac 1p \mathrm{Tr}(M_{k,t,r,s}) \beta^r_{i,0}\beta^s_{i,0}(u), \quad\mathsf R^\mathrm{lin}(u) \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \sum_{k\,=\,0}^{t-1} \sum_{r,s\,=\,0}^k \beta^r_{i,0}(u) \beta^s_{i,0}(u) \tilde m_{k,t,r,s}(u)
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    Recall from \eqref{eq:betar-betal-condition-on-Gi} that the value of $\mathbb{E}[\beta^r_{i,0}(u) \beta^s_{i,0}(u) \ | \ \mathcal{G}_i]$ does not depend on whether $u$ is $f_i$ or $\widetilde f_i$. Therefore, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb{E}[\mathsf T^\mathrm{lin}(f_i) - \mathsf T^\mathrm{lin}(\widetilde f_i) \ | \ \mathcal{G}_i] = 0
    \end{equation}
    For $\mathsf R^\mathrm{lin}$, we have from Corollary \ref{cor:scalar-bounds}
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \beta^r_{i,0}(u) \beta^s_{i,0}(u)\right\rVert_{L^2} \le C d^{-1}
    \end{equation}
    so we have
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb{E} \left\lvert \mathsf R^\mathrm{lin}(u)\right\rvert \le C d^{-1} d^{-3/2} \le C d^{-5/2}
    \end{equation}
    uniformly in $u \in \{f_i, \widetilde f_i\}$. This proves \eqref{eq:I2lin-comp}.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
Fix $k < t$. By the definitions \eqref{eq:zeta-quad-lin-def}, \eqref{eq:epsilon-quad-lin-def} of Lemma \ref{lem:epsilon-quad-lin}, \eqref{eq:U-propagator-def}, and \eqref{eq:mbar-linear-combo} of Lemma \ref{lem:m-representation}, with $\mathbf B^\ell$ defined in \eqref{eq:vector-local-def}, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \zeta^k_{\mathrm{quad},\mathrm{lin}}(u) \,=\, \sum_{\ell\,=\,0}^{k-1} \mathcal U_{\ell, k} \varepsilon^\ell_{i, \mathrm{quad}, \mathrm{lin}}(u) \,=\, \sum_{\ell \,=\, 0}^{k-1} \sum_{a,b\,=\,0}^\ell \beta^a_{i,0}(u)\beta^b_{i,0}(u) \mathcal U_{\ell, k}\mathbf B^\ell(\mathcal R_{\ell, a} u, \mathcal R_{\ell, b} u)
    \end{equation}
    where $\varepsilon^\ell_{i,\mathrm{quad}, \mathrm{lin}}$ are defined in Lemma \ref{lem:epsilon-quad-lin}. Therefore,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:Ifbmain-expand}
        \mathrm{I}^{(\mathrm{fb},\mathrm{main})}_t(u) \,=\, 2 \sum_{k\,=\,0}^{t-1} \sum_{\ell \,=\, 0}^{k - 1} \sum_{r \,=\, 0}^k \sum_{a,b\,=\,0}^\ell \beta^r_{i,0}(u) \beta^a_{i,0}(u) \beta^b_{i,0}(u) \mathfrak C_{t,k,\ell,r,a,b}(u,u,u)
    \end{equation}
    where
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak C_{t,k,\ell,r,a,b}(u_1,u_2,u_3) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \mathfrak B_{k,t}(\mathcal R_{k,r}u_1, \mathcal U_{\ell, k}\mathbf B^\ell(\mathcal R_{\ell, a}u_2, \mathcal R_{\ell, b}u_3))
    \end{equation}
    where $\mathfrak B_{k,t}$ is the bilinear form in \eqref{eq:projected-BT-def}. We claim that $\left\lVert \mathfrak C_{t,k,\ell,r,a,b}\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{HS})} \le C_m d^{1/2}$. To see this, define bilinear form on $\mathbb R^p$
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak A_{t,k,\ell,r}(x,y) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \mathfrak B_{k,t}(\mathcal R_{k,r} x, \mathcal U_{\ell, k}y)
    \end{equation}
    By \eqref{eq:scalarized-local-B-HS}, the bilinear map $\mathbf B^\ell$ satisfies \eqref{eq:hs-compose-hyp} with $K=C\sqrt d$. The composition bound \eqref{eq:hs-compose-trilinear} therefore gives
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \mathfrak C_{t,k,\ell,r,a,b}\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS} \le C \sqrt{d} \left\lVert \mathfrak B_{k,t}\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS} \left\lVert \mathcal R_{k,r}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \left\lVert \mathcal U_{\ell,k}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \left\lVert \mathcal R_{\ell,a}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \left\lVert \mathcal R_{\ell,b}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}
    \end{equation}
    So by H\"older, \eqref{eq:projected-local-HS-bound}, \eqref{eq:mbar-linear-combo} of Lemma \ref{lem:m-representation}, and \eqref{eq:U-response-bound}, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \mathfrak C_{t,k,\ell,r,a,b}\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{HS})} \le C_m d^{1/2}
    \end{equation}
    So by H\"older and \eqref{eq:mbar-linear-combo} of Lemma \ref{lem:m-representation}, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \mathfrak C\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{HS})} \,\le\, C \sqrt{d} \left\lVert \mathfrak A\right\rVert_{L^{3m}(\mathrm{HS})} \left\lVert \mathcal R_{\ell, a}\right\rVert_{L^{3m}(\mathrm{op})} \left\lVert \mathcal R_{\ell, b}\right\rVert_{L^{3m}(\mathrm{op})} \,\le\, C d^{1/2}
    \end{equation}
    Applying Lemma~\ref{lem:trilinear-decomp} to each $\mathfrak C$ gives
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak C(u,u,u) = \left\langle b,u\right\rangle + \sum_{\nu = 1}^p \left\langle e_\nu,u\right\rangle \left(u^\top M_\nu u - \frac 1p \mathrm{Tr} M_\nu \right)
    \end{equation}
    Using the fact we just proved that $\left\lVert \mathfrak C_{t,k,\ell,r,a,b}\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{HS})} \le C_m d^{1/2}$, we have from the same Lemma \ref{lem:trilinear-decomp}
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert b\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m d^{-1/2}, \quad \left\lVert \sum_{\nu \,=\, 1}^p \left\lVert M_\nu\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m d
    \end{equation}
    Therefore, we apply Corollary \ref{cor:one-column-moments} and get $\left\lVert \left\langle b,u\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^2} \le C d^{-3/2}$ and also similar to the proof of Proposition \ref{prop:projected-z-cubic-quartic}, in line \eqref{eq:Yu-L2-bound},
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \sum_{\nu = 1}^p \left\langle e_\nu,u\right\rangle\left(u^\top M_\nu u - \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr}(M_\nu) \right)\right\rVert_{L^2} \le C d^{-1}
    \end{equation}
    Since the product of the scalar $\beta$'s satisfies
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \beta^r_{i,0}(u) \beta^a_{i,0}(u) \beta^b_{i,0}(u)\right\rVert_{L^2} \le C d^{-3/2}
    \end{equation}
    we see that every term in \eqref{eq:Ifbmain-expand} has expectation of order $\mathcal{O}(d^{-5/2})$, we sum over finitely many of them and get \eqref{eq:Ifbmain-comp}.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- assumption-4: body_md -->
\label{ass:concentration-columns}
    The vectors $u_1, \ldots, u_n \in \mathbb R^p$ are independent and independent of $(S^\star, \widehat{S}^0, \epsilon)$, and satisfy
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-isotropy}
        \mathbb{E} u_i \,=\, 0, \qquad \mathbb{E} u_i u_i^\top \,=\, \frac{I_p}{p}, \qquad i \,\in\, [n].
    \end{equation}
    Further, there exists $C < \infty$ such that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-subexp}
        \sup_{i \,\le\, n} \sup_{a \,\in\, \mathbb{S}^{p - 1}} \left\lVert \sqrt{p}\, \left\langle u_i,a\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_1} \,\le\, C.
    \end{equation}
    Let
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-envelope-def}
        K_i^\mathsf{E} \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \sum_{j \ne i} u_j u_j^\top.
    \end{equation}
    Assume also that, for every integer $m \ge 1$, there exists $C_m < \infty$ independent of $d$ and $i$ such that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-envelope-moment}
        \left\lVert K_i^\mathsf{E}\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{op})} \le C_m,
    \end{equation}
    and, for every deterministic $A \in \mathbb R^{p \times p}$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-rotational}
        \left\lVert u_i^\top A u_i - \frac{1}{p} \mathrm{Tr} A\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m d^{-1/2} \left\lVert A_s\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}, \qquad A_s \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \frac{A + A^\top}{2}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end assumption-4 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{lem:moment-efron-stein}
    Let $Y \in \mathbb R^r$ and $X_i \in \mathbb R^{k_i}$, $1 \le i \le N$, be mutually independent. Let $X_1', \ldots, X_N'$ be independent copies, independent of $(Y, X_1, \ldots, X_N)$. Let
    \begin{equation}
        Z \,=\, \Phi(Y, X_1, \ldots, X_N) \,\in\, L^q, \qquad q \,\ge\, 2,
    \end{equation}
    and set
    \begin{equation}
        Z_i' = \Phi(Y, X_1, \ldots, X_{i - 1}, X_i', X_{i + 1}, \ldots, X_N).
    \end{equation}
    Define
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:conditional-replacement-variance}
        V^\pm \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \mathbb{E}\left[\left.\sum_{i = 1}^N (Z - Z_i')_\pm^2\,\right|\,Y, X_1, \ldots, X_N\right].
    \end{equation}
    For some fixed $\kappa < \infty$, we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:moment-efron-stein-one-sided}
        \left\lVert (Z - \mathbb{E}[Z \mid Y])_\pm\right\rVert_{L^q} \le \sqrt{2\kappa q}\, \left\lVert V^\pm\right\rVert_{L^{q/2}}^{1/2}.
    \end{equation}
    Consequently,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:moment-efron-stein-increments}
        \left\lVert Z - \mathbb{E}[Z \mid Y]\right\rVert_{L^q} \,\le\, 2^{1/q}\sqrt{2\kappa q}\left(\sum_{i \,=\, 1}^N \left\lVert Z - Z_i'\right\rVert_{L^q}^2\right)^{1/2}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
Because the joint law is a product law, Fubini's theorem implies that, for almost every $y$,
    \begin{equation}
        Z_y \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \Phi(y, X_1, \ldots, X_N)
    \end{equation}
    belongs to $L^q$ under the law of $(X_1, \ldots, X_N)$. For such $y$, the second inequality in each one-sided bound of \cite[Theorem 2]{BoucheronStephane2005MIfF} gives
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert (Z_y - \mathbb{E}_X Z_y)_\pm\right\rVert_{L^q(X)} \le \sqrt{2\kappa q}\, \left\lVert V_y^\pm\right\rVert_{L^{q/2}(X)}^{1/2},
    \end{equation}
    where $V_y^\pm$ is the section of \eqref{eq:conditional-replacement-variance} at $Y = y$ and $L^q(X)$ denotes the space where we take expectation over the randomness of $X = (X_1, \ldots, X_N)$. Raising this inequality to the $q$th power, integrating with respect to $y$, and using Fubini's theorem gives \eqref{eq:moment-efron-stein-one-sided}.

    Since $q/2 \ge 1$, conditional Jensen's inequality and Minkowski's inequality give
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \left\lVert V^\pm\right\rVert_{L^{q/2}} &\le \sum_{i = 1}^N \left\lVert \mathbb{E}[(Z - Z_i')_\pm^2 \mid Y, X_1, \ldots, X_N]\right\rVert_{L^{q/2}} \\
            &\le \sum_{i = 1}^N \left\lVert Z - Z_i'\right\rVert_{L^q}^2.
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    Finally,
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert Z - \mathbb{E}[Z \mid Y]\right\rVert_{L^q}^q = \left\lVert (Z - \mathbb{E}[Z \mid Y])_+\right\rVert_{L^q}^q + \left\lVert (Z - \mathbb{E}[Z \mid Y])_-\right\rVert_{L^q}^q.
    \end{equation}
    Applying \eqref{eq:moment-efron-stein-one-sided} to both signs and using the preceding bound on $V^\pm$ proves \eqref{eq:moment-efron-stein-increments}.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{lem:one-vector-sensitivity}
    Under Assumptions \ref{ass:basic}, \ref{ass:signal-init}, and \ref{ass:concentration-columns}, let $u \stackrel{d}{=} u_i$ and $\eta\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\Delta)$ be independent of each other and jointly independent of $\mathcal H_i^\mathsf{E}$. For every integer $m\geq1$, uniformly in $i$ and $0\leq s,t\leq T$, we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-q-moment}
        \left\lVert q_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_m d^{-1/2}.
    \end{equation}
    If $\lambda_i \in \mathbb R^p$ is $\mathcal H_i^\mathsf{E}$-measurable and $\left\lVert \lambda_i\right\rVert \le L d^{-1/2}$ a.s., then
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-projected-linear}
        \left\lVert \left\langle \lambda_i,q_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_{m,L} d^{-3/2}.
    \end{equation}
    If $H_i \in \mathbb R^{p \times p}$ is $\mathcal H_i^\mathsf{E}$-measurable and $\left\lVert H_i\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \le L d^{-1}$ a.s., then
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-projected-quadratic}
        \left\lVert \left\langle q_i^t(u,\eta),H_i q_i^s(u,\eta)\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_{m,L} d^{-2}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
The proof is essentially the same as Proposition \ref{prop:exact-loo} and Section \ref{sec:linearized}, and is deferred to later in this section.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{thm:concentration-general-ensemble}
    Under Assumptions \ref{ass:basic}, \ref{ass:signal-init}, and \ref{ass:concentration-columns}, fix $0\leq s,t\leq T$ and $\mu\in\mathscr{C}^3(\mathbb R)$. Define
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-observables}
        \Phi_t^\mathrm{sig} \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \frac{1}{d} \mathrm{Tr}(\widehat{S}_\mathsf{E}^t S^\star), \qquad \Phi_{t,\mu}^\mathrm{spec} \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \frac{1}{d} \mathrm{Tr}(\mu(\widehat{S}_\mathsf{E}^t)), \qquad \Phi_{s,t}^\mathrm{cross} \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \frac{1}{d} \mathrm{Tr}(\widehat{S}_\mathsf{E}^s \widehat{S}_\mathsf{E}^t).
    \end{equation}
    For every integer $m \ge 2$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-observables-moment}
        \max_{\Phi \,\in\, \{\Phi_t^\mathrm{sig},\Phi_{t,\mu}^\mathrm{spec},\Phi_{s,t}^\mathrm{cross}\}} \left\lVert \Phi - \mathbb{E}[\Phi \mid \mathcal B_0]\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_{m,\mu} d^{-1/2}.
    \end{equation}
    Consequently,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-observables-stochastic}
        \Phi - \mathbb{E}[\Phi \mid \mathcal B_0] = \mathcal{O}_\prec(d^{-1/2})
    \end{equation}
    for every observable in \eqref{eq:concentration-observables}.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Let
    \begin{equation}
        Y \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, (\operatorname{vec}(S^\star), \operatorname{vec}(\widehat{S}^0)) \,\in\, \mathbb R^{2p}, \qquad X_i \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, (u_i, \epsilon_i).
    \end{equation}
    The random vectors $Y, X_1, \ldots, X_n$ are mutually independent. Let $X_i' = (u_i', \epsilon_i')$ be an independent copy of $X_i$. For any one of the observables in \eqref{eq:concentration-observables}, write $\Phi_i(u,\eta)$ for the observable generated by $(F_i^\mathsf{E}(u), \epsilon^{(i,\eta)})$, and write $\Phi_{\setminus i}$ for the observable generated by $(F_{\setminus i}^\mathsf{E}, \epsilon^{(i,0)})$. Then
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-signal-increment}
        \Phi_{t,i}^\mathrm{sig}(u,\eta) - \Phi_{t,{\setminus i}}^\mathrm{sig} = \left\langle d^{-1}{\mathbf s}^*,q_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rangle.
    \end{equation}
    The cross-time correlation satisfies
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-cross-increment}
        \Phi_{s,t,i}^\mathrm{cross}(u,\eta) - \Phi_{s,t,{\setminus i}}^\mathrm{cross} = \left\langle d^{-1}\widehat{\mathbf s}_{\setminus i}^s,q_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rangle + \left\langle d^{-1}\widehat{\mathbf s}_{\setminus i}^t,q_i^s(u,\eta)\right\rangle + \left\langle q_i^s(u,\eta),d^{-1}q_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rangle.
    \end{equation}
    To expand the spectral statistic, use Lemma \ref{lem:base-det-bounds} to localize Lemma \ref{lem:smooth-spectral-expansion} to the deterministic spectral interval and set
    \begin{equation}
        A_i \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \widehat{S}_{\setminus i}^t, \qquad E_i(u,\eta) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \text{mat}(q_i^t(u,\eta)).
    \end{equation}
    Then
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-spectral-increment}
        \Phi_{t,\mu,i}^\mathrm{spec}(u,\eta) - \Phi_{t,\mu,{\setminus i}}^\mathrm{spec} = \left\langle d^{-1}\operatorname{vec}(\mu'(A_i)),q_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rangle + \left\langle q_i^t(u,\eta),K_{\mu,A_i}q_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rangle + R_{\mu,A_i}(E_i(u,\eta)),
    \end{equation}
    where
    \begin{equation}
        d^{-1}\left\lVert \mu'(A_i)\right\rVert_\mathrm{F} \,\le\, \left\lVert \mu'\right\rVert_{L^\infty(I_\ast)}d^{-1/2}, \qquad \left\lVert K_{\mu,A_i}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \,\le\, C_\mu d^{-1},
    \end{equation}
    and, by \eqref{eq:smooth-spectral-remainder-bound} of Lemma \ref{lem:smooth-spectral-expansion} and \eqref{eq:concentration-q-moment} of Lemma \ref{lem:one-vector-sensitivity},
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-spectral-remainder}
        \left\lVert R_{\mu,A_i}(E_i(u,\eta))\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_\mu d^{-1}\left\lVert q_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rVert_{L^{3m}}^3 \le C_{m,\mu} d^{-5/2}.
    \end{equation}

    By Assumption \ref{ass:signal-init} and Lemma \ref{lem:base-det-bounds},
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert d^{-1}{\mathbf s}^*\right\rVert + \max_{r \le T}\left\lVert d^{-1}\widehat{\mathbf s}_{\setminus i}^r\right\rVert \le C d^{-1/2}
    \end{equation}
    almost surely. Therefore, applying Lemma \ref{lem:one-vector-sensitivity} to \eqref{eq:concentration-signal-increment}--\eqref{eq:concentration-spectral-increment}, with $H_i = d^{-1}I_p$ for the last term in \eqref{eq:concentration-cross-increment}, gives
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-one-block-increment}
        \max_{i \,\le\, n}\max_{\Phi \,\in\, \{\Phi_t^\mathrm{sig},\Phi_{t,\mu}^\mathrm{spec},\Phi_{s,t}^\mathrm{cross}\}} \left\lVert \Phi_i(u,\eta) - \Phi_{\setminus i}\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_{m,\mu} d^{-3/2}.
    \end{equation}
    Here the quadratic terms are in fact $\mathcal{O}_{L^m}(d^{-2})$, and the spectral remainder is $\mathcal{O}_{L^m}(d^{-5/2})$.

    Write \eqref{eq:concentration-one-block-increment} once with $(u,\eta) = (u_i,\epsilon_i)$ and once with $(u,\eta) = (u_i',\epsilon_i')$. Since both pairs are independent of $\mathcal H_i^\mathsf{E}$, the triangle inequality gives
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-resampling-increment}
        \max_{i \,\le\, n}\max_{\Phi \,\in\, \{\Phi_t^\mathrm{sig},\Phi_{t,\mu}^\mathrm{spec},\Phi_{s,t}^\mathrm{cross}\}} \left\lVert \Phi - \Phi^{(i)}\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_{m,\mu} d^{-3/2},
    \end{equation}
    where $\Phi^{(i)}$ is generated with $X_i'$ in place of $X_i$.

    The observables in \eqref{eq:concentration-observables} are bounded uniformly in $d$: this follows from Assumption \ref{ass:signal-init}, Lemma \ref{lem:base-det-bounds}, and the bound of $\mu$ on the deterministic spectral interval. Hence they belong to $L^m$ for every $m$. Applying \eqref{eq:moment-efron-stein-increments} of Lemma \ref{lem:moment-efron-stein} to $Y, X_1, \ldots, X_n$, and using $n\leq C_0p$ and $p\asymp d^2$, gives
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \left\lVert \Phi - \mathbb{E}[\Phi \mid \mathcal B_0]\right\rVert_{L^m} &\le\, 2^{1/m}\sqrt{2\kappa m}\left(\sum_{i \,=\, 1}^n \left\lVert \Phi - \Phi^{(i)}\right\rVert_{L^m}^2\right)^{1/2} \\
            &\le C_{m,\mu}\sqrt{n}\, d^{-3/2} \le C_{m,\mu} d^{-1/2}.
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    This proves \eqref{eq:concentration-observables-moment}. For $\varepsilon, D > 0$, choose an integer $m \ge 2$ such that $m\varepsilon > D$. Markov's inequality gives
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb P\left(\left\lvert \Phi - \mathbb{E}[\Phi \mid \mathcal B_0]\right\rvert \,>\, d^{-1/2 + \varepsilon}\right) \,\le\, C_{m,\mu}^m d^{-m\varepsilon} \,\le\, d^{-D}
    \end{equation}
    for all sufficiently large $d$. This proves \eqref{eq:concentration-observables-stochastic}.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- corollary-6: body_md -->
\label{cor:concentration-goe-rank-one}
    Under Assumptions \ref{ass:basic} and \ref{ass:signal-init}, Theorem \ref{thm:concentration-general-ensemble} holds for both $F^\mathsf{E} = F$ and $F^\mathsf{E} = \widetilde F$.
<!-- end corollary-6 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
The proof is almost identical to that of Lemma \ref{lem:sensing-envelope} and is deferred to later in this section.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- corollary-7: body_md -->
\label{cor:concentration-consequences}
    Under the hypotheses of Corollary \ref{cor:concentration-goe-rank-one}, for every fixed integer $k \ge 1$, every $0 \le t \le T$, and $\mathsf E \in \{\mathrm{RO}, \mathrm{G}\}$,
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{d}\mathrm{Tr}((\widehat{S}_{\mathsf E}^t)^k) - \mathbb{E}\left[\left.\frac{1}{d}\mathrm{Tr}((\widehat{S}_{\mathsf E}^t)^k)\,\right|\,\mathcal B_0\right] = \mathcal{O}_\prec(d^{-1/2}),
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{d}\left\lVert \widehat{S}_{\mathsf E}^t - S^\star\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2 - \mathbb{E}\left[\left.\frac{1}{d}\left\lVert \widehat{S}_{\mathsf E}^t - S^\star\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2\,\right|\,\mathcal B_0\right] = \mathcal{O}_\prec(d^{-1/2}).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end corollary-7 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
By Lemma \ref{lem:base-det-bounds}, the cutoff $\mu_{k,\ast}$ from the proof of Corollary \ref{cor:power-from-spectral} satisfies $\mu_{k,\ast}(\widehat{S}_{\mathsf E}^t) = (\widehat{S}_{\mathsf E}^t)^k$. The first claim therefore follows from Theorem \ref{thm:concentration-general-ensemble}. For the MSE,
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{d}\left\lVert \widehat{S}_{\mathsf E}^t - S^\star\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2 = \Phi_{t,t}^\mathrm{cross} - 2\Phi_t^\mathrm{sig} + \frac{1}{d}\left\lVert S^\star\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2.
    \end{equation}
    Subtracting conditional expectations, the last term cancels and gives
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{1}{d}\left\lVert \widehat{S}_{\mathsf E}^t - S^\star\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2 - \mathbb{E}\left[\left.\frac{1}{d}\left\lVert \widehat{S}_{\mathsf E}^t - S^\star\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2\,\right|\,\mathcal B_0\right] = \Phi_{t,t}^\mathrm{cross} - \mathbb{E}[\Phi_{t,t}^\mathrm{cross} \mid \mathcal B_0] - 2(\Phi_t^\mathrm{sig} - \mathbb{E}[\Phi_t^\mathrm{sig} \mid \mathcal B_0]).
    \end{equation}
    The second claim now follows from Theorem \ref{thm:concentration-general-ensemble}.
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
Conditioning on $\mathcal H_i^\mathsf{E}$, \eqref{eq:concentration-subexp} gives, for every $\mathcal H_i^\mathsf{E}$-measurable $a \in \mathbb R^p$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-linear-moment}
        \left\lVert \left\langle u,a\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_m p^{-1/2}\left\lVert a\right\rVert_{L^m}.
    \end{equation}
    Indeed, $\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{L^m} \le (2m!)^{1/m}\left\lVert X\right\rVert_{\psi_1}$. For $m \ge 2$, Minkowski's inequality and \eqref{eq:concentration-subexp} also give
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert u\right\rVert_{L^m}^2 \,=\, \left\lVert \left\lVert u\right\rVert^2\right\rVert_{L^{m/2}} \,\le\, \sum_{j \,=\, 1}^p \left\lVert \left\langle u,e_j\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^m}^2 \,\le\, C_m,
    \end{equation}
    and the same bound for $1 \le m < 2$ follows by monotonicity of the moment norms. The same conditioning extends \eqref{eq:concentration-rotational} to every $\mathcal H_i^\mathsf{E}$-measurable matrix $A$:
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-random-rotational}
        \left\lVert u^\top A u - \frac{1}{p}\mathrm{Tr} A\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_m d^{-1/2}\left\lVert A_s\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{op})}.
    \end{equation}

    Define, for $0 \le t \le T - 1$,
    \begin{equation}
        w_i^t(u,\eta) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= h_i^t(u,\eta) - h_{\setminus i}^t, \qquad m_i^t(u,\eta) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= q_i^t(u,\eta) + a_t w_i^t(u,\eta).
    \end{equation}
    The same calculation as in Proposition \ref{prop:exact-loo} gives
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-exact-loo}
        w_i^t(u,\eta) \,=\, -\kappa_t K_i^\mathsf{E} q_i^t(u,\eta) + \rho_t w_i^{t - 1}(u,\eta) + u\beta_i^t(u,\eta), \qquad \beta_i^t(u,\eta) \,=\, \kappa_t\left\langle u,{\mathbf s}^* - \widehat{\mathbf s}_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rangle + \nu_t\eta.
    \end{equation}
    Let
    \begin{equation}
        y_i^t \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \widehat{\mathbf s}_{\setminus i}^t + a_t h_{\setminus i}^t, \qquad L_i^t \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= DG_t(y_i^t).
    \end{equation}
    Then
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-q-taylor}
        q_i^{t + 1}(u,\eta) \,=\, L_i^t m_i^t(u,\eta) + \varepsilon_i^t(u,\eta), \qquad \left\lVert L_i^t\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \,\le\, C, \qquad \left\lVert \varepsilon_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rVert \,\le\, C\left\lVert m_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rVert^2,
    \end{equation}
    where
    \begin{equation}
        \varepsilon_i^t(u,\eta) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= G_t(y_i^t + m_i^t(u,\eta)) - G_t(y_i^t) - DG_t(y_i^t)[m_i^t(u,\eta)].
    \end{equation}
    We suppress the dependence on $(u,\eta)$ below. By Assumption \ref{ass:signal-init}, Lemma \ref{lem:base-det-bounds}, \eqref{eq:concentration-linear-moment}, and $p \asymp d^2$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-beta-zero}
        \left\lVert \kappa_t\left\langle u,{\mathbf s}^* - \widehat{\mathbf s}_{\setminus i}^t\right\rangle + \nu_t\eta\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_m d^{-1/2}.
    \end{equation}
    Since
    \begin{equation}
        \beta_i^t = \kappa_t\left\langle u,{\mathbf s}^* - \widehat{\mathbf s}_{\setminus i}^t\right\rangle - \kappa_t\left\langle u,q_i^t\right\rangle + \nu_t\eta,
    \end{equation}
    H\"older's inequality gives
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \beta_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_m d^{-1/2} + C_m\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{L^{2m}}\left\lVert q_i^t\right\rVert_{L^{2m}},
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert w_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_m\left\lVert K_i^\mathsf{E}\right\rVert_{L^{2m}(\mathrm{op})}\left\lVert q_i^t\right\rVert_{L^{2m}} + C_m\left\lVert w_i^{t - 1}\right\rVert_{L^m} + C_m\left\lVert u\right\rVert_{L^{2m}}\left\lVert \beta_i^t\right\rVert_{L^{2m}}.
    \end{equation}
    Furthermore,
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert q_i^{t + 1}\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C\left\lVert m_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} + C\left\lVert m_i^t\right\rVert_{L^{2m}}^2, \qquad \left\lVert m_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, \left\lVert q_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} + C\left\lVert w_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m}.
    \end{equation}
    Starting from $q_i^0 = 0$ and $w_i^{-1} = 0$, we apply these inequalities simultaneously at every moment order. Induction on $t$, using \eqref{eq:concentration-envelope-moment}, gives
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-basic-sensitivity}
        \max_{0 \,\le\, t \,\le\, T}\left\lVert q_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} + \max_{0 \,\le\, t \,\le\, T - 1}\left(\left\lVert w_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} + \left\lVert m_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} + \left\lVert \beta_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m}\right) \,\le\, C_m d^{-1/2},
    \end{equation}
    and hence
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-epsilon-size}
        \max_{0 \,\le\, t \,\le\, T - 1}\left\lVert \varepsilon_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m d^{-1}.
    \end{equation}
    This proves \eqref{eq:concentration-q-moment}.

    To prove \eqref{eq:concentration-projected-linear}, define, for $0 \le t \le T - 1$,
    \begin{equation}
        \delta_i^t \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \begin{pmatrix} q_i^t \\ w_i^{t - 1} \end{pmatrix}, \qquad M_i^t \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \begin{pmatrix} L_i^t(I_p - a_t \kappa_t K_i^\mathsf{E}) & a_t \rho_t L_i^t \\ -\kappa_t K_i^\mathsf{E} & \rho_t I_p \end{pmatrix}, \qquad B_i^t \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \begin{pmatrix} a_t L_i^t \\ I_p \end{pmatrix}.
    \end{equation}
    Equations \eqref{eq:concentration-exact-loo} and \eqref{eq:concentration-q-taylor} are equivalently
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-block-recursion}
        \delta_i^{t + 1} = M_i^t \delta_i^t + B_i^t u \beta_i^t + \begin{pmatrix} \varepsilon_i^t \\ 0 \end{pmatrix}.
    \end{equation}
    Let $\Pi_1 : \mathbb R^{2p} \to \mathbb R^p$ be the projection onto the first block. For $0 \le k < t \le T$, define
    \begin{equation}
        P_{k,t}^{(i)} \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \Pi_1 M_i^{t - 1}\cdots M_i^{k + 1}B_i^k,
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        z_i^t(u,\eta) \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \Pi_1\sum_{\ell \,=\, 0}^{t - 1} M_i^{t - 1}\cdots M_i^{\ell + 1}\begin{pmatrix} \varepsilon_i^\ell(u,\eta) \\ 0 \end{pmatrix}, \qquad z_i^0 \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, 0.
    \end{equation}
    Empty products are understood as the identity. Expanding \eqref{eq:concentration-exact-loo} and \eqref{eq:concentration-q-taylor} gives
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-unrolled}
        q_i^t(u,\eta) = \sum_{k = 0}^{t - 1} P_{k,t}^{(i)}u\beta_i^k(u,\eta) + z_i^t(u,\eta).
    \end{equation}
    The operators $M_i^t$, $B_i^t$, and $P_{k,t}^{(i)}$ are $\mathcal H_i^\mathsf{E}$-measurable. By \eqref{eq:concentration-envelope-moment} and \eqref{eq:concentration-epsilon-size}, H\"older's inequality gives
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-response-bounds}
        \max_{0 \,\le\, k \,<\, t \,\le\, T}\left\lVert P_{k,t}^{(i)}\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{op})} \,\le\, C_m, \qquad \max_{0 \,\le\, t \,\le\, T}\left\lVert z_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m d^{-1}.
    \end{equation}
    Set
    \begin{equation}
        \beta_{i,0}^t(u,\eta) \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \kappa_t\left\langle u,{\mathbf s}^* - \widehat{\mathbf s}_{\setminus i}^t\right\rangle + \nu_t\eta, \qquad \Omega_{k,t} \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \kappa_t u^\top P_{k,t}^{(i)}u, \qquad \eta_i^t \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \kappa_t\left\langle u,z_i^t\right\rangle.
    \end{equation}
    Then
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-beta-recursion}
        \beta_i^t = \beta_{i,0}^t - \sum_{k < t}\Omega_{k,t}\beta_i^k - \eta_i^t.
    \end{equation}
    Define
    \begin{equation}
        \overline\Omega_{k,t} \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \frac{\kappa_t}{p}\mathrm{Tr} P_{k,t}^{(i)}.
    \end{equation}
    By \eqref{eq:concentration-random-rotational}, \eqref{eq:concentration-response-bounds}, and H\"older's inequality,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-kernel-bounds}
        \left\lVert \Omega_{k,t} - \overline\Omega_{k,t}\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m d^{-1/2}, \qquad \left\lVert \overline\Omega_{k,t}\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m, \qquad \left\lVert \eta_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m d^{-1}.
    \end{equation}
    Define
    \begin{equation}
        \overline\alpha_i^t \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \beta_{i,0}^t - \sum_{k < t}\overline\Omega_{k,t}\overline\alpha_i^k.
    \end{equation}
    Since $T$ is fixed, there exist $\mathcal H_i^\mathsf{E}$-measurable coefficients $c_{t,r}$ such that
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-alpha-expansion}
        \overline\alpha_i^t \,=\, \sum_{r \,=\, 0}^t c_{t,r}\beta_{i,0}^r, \qquad \max_{r \,\le\, t}\left\lVert c_{t,r}\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m.
    \end{equation}
    With $d_i^t \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \beta_i^t - \overline\alpha_i^t$, subtracting the last recursion from \eqref{eq:concentration-beta-recursion} gives
    \begin{equation}
        d_i^t \,=\, -\sum_{k \,<\, t}\overline\Omega_{k,t}d_i^k - \sum_{k \,<\, t}(\Omega_{k,t} - \overline\Omega_{k,t})\beta_i^k - \eta_i^t.
    \end{equation}
    Applying this recursion simultaneously at every moment order, \eqref{eq:concentration-basic-sensitivity} and \eqref{eq:concentration-kernel-bounds} give by induction
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-amplitude-error}
        \max_{0 \,\le\, t \,\le\, T - 1}\left\lVert d_i^t\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m d^{-1}.
    \end{equation}
    Define
    \begin{equation}
        B_{t,r}^{(i)} \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \sum_{k \,=\, r}^{t - 1} c_{k,r}P_{k,t}^{(i)}, \qquad e_i^t \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, z_i^t + \sum_{k \,=\, 0}^{t - 1} P_{k,t}^{(i)}u d_i^k.
    \end{equation}
    From \eqref{eq:concentration-unrolled} and \eqref{eq:concentration-alpha-expansion},
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-q-expansion}
        q_i^t(u,\eta) = \sum_{r = 0}^{t - 1} B_{t,r}^{(i)}u\beta_{i,0}^r(u,\eta) + e_i^t(u,\eta),
    \end{equation}
    and \eqref{eq:concentration-response-bounds}, \eqref{eq:concentration-amplitude-error}, and H\"older's inequality give
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:concentration-q-expansion-bounds}
        \max_{0 \,\le\, r \,<\, t \,\le\, T}\left\lVert B_{t,r}^{(i)}\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{op})} \,\le\, C_m, \qquad \max_{0 \,\le\, t \,\le\, T}\left\lVert e_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, C_m d^{-1}.
    \end{equation}
    For $t = 0$, \eqref{eq:concentration-projected-linear} is immediate. For $1 \le t \le T$, \eqref{eq:concentration-beta-zero}, \eqref{eq:concentration-linear-moment}, H\"older's inequality, and \eqref{eq:concentration-q-expansion} give
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
            \left\lVert \left\langle \lambda_i,q_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^m} &\le\, L d^{-1/2}\left\lVert e_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rVert_{L^m} + \sum_{r \,<\, t}\left\lVert \left\langle u,(B_{t,r}^{(i)})^\top\lambda_i\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^{2m}}\left\lVert \beta_{i,0}^r(u,\eta)\right\rVert_{L^{2m}} \\
            &\le C_{m,L} d^{-3/2} + C_{m,L} d^{-2} \le C_{m,L} d^{-3/2}.
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    This proves \eqref{eq:concentration-projected-linear}. Finally, \eqref{eq:concentration-q-moment} and H\"older's inequality give
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \left\langle q_i^t(u,\eta),H_i q_i^s(u,\eta)\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^m} \,\le\, L d^{-1}\left\lVert q_i^t(u,\eta)\right\rVert_{L^{2m}}\left\lVert q_i^s(u,\eta)\right\rVert_{L^{2m}} \,\le\, C_{m,L} d^{-2},
    \end{equation}
    which proves \eqref{eq:concentration-projected-quadratic}.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
Independence and isotropy are given by \eqref{eq:column-covariance}. Fix $a \in \mathbb{S}^{p - 1}$ and let $M = \text{mat}(a)$, so $\left\lVert M\right\rVert_\mathrm{F} = 1$. For the GOE column,
    \begin{equation}
        \sqrt{p}\,\left\langle \widetilde f_i,a\right\rangle \sim \mathcal{N}(0,1).
    \end{equation}
    For the rank-one column,
    \begin{equation}
        \sqrt{p}\,\left\langle f_i,a\right\rangle = \frac{x_i^\top Mx_i - \mathrm{Tr} M}{\sqrt{2}}.
    \end{equation}
    If $(\lambda_j)_{j = 1}^d$ are the eigenvalues of $M$, then $\max_j\left\lvert \lambda_j\right\rvert \le 1$. For $\left\lvert t\right\rvert \le 1/4$,
    \begin{equation}
        \log \mathbb{E}\exp\left(t(x_i^\top Mx_i - \mathrm{Tr} M)\right) \,=\, \sum_{j \,=\, 1}^d\left(-t\lambda_j - \frac{1}{2}\log(1 - 2t\lambda_j)\right) \,\le\, 2t^2\sum_{j \,=\, 1}^d\lambda_j^2 \,=\, 2t^2.
    \end{equation}
    Here we used $-z - \log(1 - z) \le z^2$ for $\left\lvert z\right\rvert \le 1/2$. Chernoff's bound, with $t = s/4$ for $0 \le s \le 1$ and $t = 1/4$ for $s \ge 1$, gives
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-one-linear-tail}
        \mathbb P\left(\left\lvert x_i^\top Mx_i - \mathrm{Tr} M\right\rvert > s\right) \le 2\exp\left(-\frac{1}{8}\min\{s^2,s\}\right).
    \end{equation}
    Integrating \eqref{eq:rank-one-linear-tail} gives $\left\lVert x_i^\top Mx_i - \mathrm{Tr} M\right\rVert_{\psi_1} \le C$. Therefore, \eqref{eq:concentration-subexp} holds for both ensembles.

    We verify \eqref{eq:concentration-envelope-moment}. The first calculation in the proof of Lemma \ref{lem:sensing-envelope} gives
    \begin{equation}
        p\left\lVert \widetilde f_i\right\rVert^2 \sim \chi_p^2, \qquad \left\lVert f_i\right\rVert^2 = \frac{\left\lVert x_i\right\rVert^4 - 2\left\lVert x_i\right\rVert^2 + d}{d(d + 1)},
    \end{equation}
    and hence $\left\lVert u_i\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_m$ in both cases. Let
    \begin{equation}
        V_i \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, (u_j)_{j \ne i} \,\in\, \mathbb R^{p \times n-1}, \qquad X_j \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \sqrt{p}\,u_j, \qquad N \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, n - 1.
    \end{equation}
    The vectors $X_j$ are centered and isotropic. For $L \ge 2$, Hanson--Wright and the preceding norm formulas give
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb P(\left\lVert \widetilde f_j\right\rVert \,>\, L) \,\le\, e^{-c_Lp}, \qquad \mathbb P(\left\lVert f_j\right\rVert \,>\, L) \,\le\, \mathbb P(\chi_d^2 \,>\, Ld/2) \,\le\, e^{-c_Ld},
    \end{equation}
    where $c_L \to \infty$ as $L \to \infty$. Since $N/p$ stays bounded above, a union bound and a sufficiently large fixed $L$ give
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb P\left(\max_{j \ne i}\frac{\left\lVert X_j\right\rVert}{\sqrt{p}} \,>\, L\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{N}{p}\right)^{1/4}\right\}\right) \,\le\, e^{-\sqrt{p}}
    \end{equation}
    for all sufficiently large $d$, uniformly in $i$. Thus, when $N \ge p$, Theorem 1 of \cite{1012.0294} and isotropy give
    \begin{equation}
        N^{-1}\left\lVert (X_j)_{j \ne i}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2 \le 1 + C\sqrt{p/N},
    \end{equation}
    while, when $N < p$, Remark 2 following that theorem gives $\left\lVert (X_j)_{j \ne i}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \le C\sqrt{p}$. Since $V_i = p^{-1/2}[X_j]_{j \ne i}$ and $K_i^\mathsf{E} = V_iV_i^\top$, both cases give
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:pure-ensemble-operator-tail}
        \mathbb P\left(\left\lVert K_i^\mathsf{E}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} > C\right) \le 2e^{-c\sqrt{p}}.
    \end{equation}
    Moreover,
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert K_i^\mathsf{E}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \,\le\, \mathrm{Tr} K_i^\mathsf{E} \,=\, \sum_{j \ne i}\left\lVert u_j\right\rVert^2, \qquad \left\lVert \mathrm{Tr} K_i^\mathsf{E}\right\rVert_{L^{2m}} \,\le\, \sum_{j \ne i}\left\lVert u_j\right\rVert_{L^{4m}}^2 \,\le\, C_m n \,\le\, C_m d^2.
    \end{equation}
    If $\mathcal E_i = \{\left\lVert K_i^\mathsf{E}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \le C\}$, then Cauchy--Schwarz and \eqref{eq:pure-ensemble-operator-tail} give
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb{E}\left\lVert K_i^\mathsf{E}\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^m \,\le\, C^m + \mathbb{E}[(\mathrm{Tr} K_i^\mathsf{E})^m\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal E_i^c}] \,\le\, C^m + \left\lVert \mathrm{Tr} K_i^\mathsf{E}\right\rVert_{L^{2m}}^m\mathbb P(\mathcal E_i^c)^{1/2} \,\le\, C_m.
    \end{equation}
    This proves \eqref{eq:concentration-envelope-moment}. Finally, \eqref{eq:quadratic-moment-lm} of Lemma \ref{lem:one-column-moments}, applied to deterministic $A$, gives \eqref{eq:concentration-rotational}. Therefore, Assumption \ref{ass:concentration-columns} holds for both sensing ensembles.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{lem:dk-bounds}
    Let $m\in\{1,2,3,4\}$ and let $\varphi\in C^m(\mathbb R)$. Define
    $\Sigma(M)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \varphi(M)$ on $\textsf{Sym}_d$ and
    \begin{equation}
        G(v)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \operatorname{vec}\big(\Sigma(\mathrm{mat}(v))\big).
    \end{equation}
    Then $\Sigma$ and $G$ are $m$-times Fr\'echet-differentiable. If
    $X=\sum_{a=1}^d \lambda_a P_a$ is an orthogonal spectral decomposition of
    $X\in\textsf{Sym}_d$ into rank-one spectral projectors (with repeated eigenvalues
    listed with multiplicity), then
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:dk-formula}
        D^m\Sigma(X)[H_1,\dots,H_m]
        =
        \sum_{\pi\in S_m}
        \sum_{i_0,\dots,i_m=1}^d
        \varphi^{[m]}(\lambda_{i_0},\dots,\lambda_{i_m})
        P_{i_0}H_{\pi(1)}P_{i_1}\cdots H_{\pi(m)}P_{i_m}.
    \end{equation}
    If in addition $\left\lVert \varphi^{(m)}\right\rVert_\infty<\infty$, then
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:dk-frobenius-bound}
        \left\lVert D^m\Sigma(X)[H_1,\dots,H_m]\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}
        \le \left\lVert \varphi^{(m)}\right\rVert_\infty \prod_{r=1}^m \left\lVert H_r\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}},
    \end{equation}
    and therefore
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:dk-vector-bound}
        \left\lVert D^m G(x)[h_1,\dots,h_m]\right\rVert
        \le \left\lVert \varphi^{(m)}\right\rVert_\infty \prod_{r=1}^m \left\lVert h_r\right\rVert.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{lem:spectral-taylor}
    Let $\varphi\in C_b^2(\mathbb R)$, i.e.
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \varphi'\right\rVert_\infty+\left\lVert \varphi''\right\rVert_\infty<\infty.
    \end{equation}
    Define $G(v)=\operatorname{vec}(\varphi(\mathrm{mat}(v)))$. Then $G:\mathbb R^p\to\mathbb R^p$ is
    Fr\'echet differentiable. Moreover, for every $x,h\in\mathbb R^p$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:G-lipschitz}
        \left\lVert DG(x)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}
        \le \left\lVert \varphi'\right\rVert_\infty,
    \end{equation}
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:G-second}
        \left\lVert G(x+h)-G(x)-DG(x)h\right\rVert
        \le \frac12 \left\lVert \varphi''\right\rVert_\infty\left\lVert h\right\rVert^2.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-32: body_md -->
By Lemma \ref{lem:dk-bounds} with $m=1,2$, the map $G$ is $C^2$ and
    satisfies
    \begin{equation}
        \sup_{z\in\mathbb R^p}\left\lVert DG(z)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\le \left\lVert \varphi'\right\rVert_\infty,
        \qquad
        \sup_{z\in\mathbb R^p}\left\lVert D^2G(z)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\le \left\lVert \varphi''\right\rVert_\infty.
    \end{equation}
    This proves \eqref{eq:G-lipschitz}. Taylor's formula with integral
    remainder in the finite-dimensional Banach space $\mathbb R^p$ gives
    \begin{equation}
        G(x+h)-G(x)-DG(x)h
        =\int_0^1(1-s)D^2G(x+sh)[h,h]ds.
    \end{equation}
    Taking norms gives \eqref{eq:G-second}.
<!-- end proof-32 -->
<!-- lemma-11: body_md -->
\label{lem:smooth-spectral-expansion}
    Fix $L<\infty$ and $\psi\in\mathscr{C}^3(\mathbb R)$, and put
    \begin{equation}
        \Phi_\psi(A)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \frac1d\mathrm{Tr}\big(\psi(A)\big),
        \qquad A\in\textsf{Sym}_d.
    \end{equation}
    There exists $C_{\psi,L}<\infty$, depending only on $L$ and on the suprema of $\psi$ and its first three derivatives over $[-L-1,L+1]$, such that the following holds for all $A,E\in\textsf{Sym}_d$ satisfying
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:smooth-spectral-local-domain}
        \max\{\|A\|_\mathrm{op},\|A+E\|_\mathrm{op}\}\leq L.
    \end{equation}
    We have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:smooth-spectral-taylor}
        \Phi_\psi(A + E) - \Phi_\psi(A) = \frac{1}{d} \mathrm{Tr}(\psi'(A) E) + Q_{\psi, A}(E) + R_{\psi, A}(E),
    \end{equation}
    where $Q_{\psi, A}$ is a quadratic form represented by a self-adjoint linear
    operator $\mathcal K_{\psi, A}:\textsf{Sym}_d \to \textsf{Sym}_d$ with
    \begin{equation}
        Q_{\psi, A}(E) = \langle E, \mathcal K_{\psi, A} E \rangle
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        \|\mathcal K_{\psi, A}\|_{F \to F} \leq C_{\psi,L} d^{-1}.
    \end{equation}
    The remainder satisfies
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:smooth-spectral-remainder-bound}
        |R_{\psi, A}(E)| \leq C_{\psi,L} d^{-1} \|E\|_\mathrm{F}^3.
    \end{equation}
    Equivalently, in vector form, there is a symmetric matrix
    $K_{\psi, A} \in \mathbb R^{p \times p}$ such that
    \begin{equation}
        Q_{\psi, A}(E) = {\operatorname{vec}(E)}^\top K_{\psi, A} \operatorname{vec}(E),
        \qquad
        \|K_{\psi, A}\|_\mathrm{op} \leq C_{\psi,L} d^{-1}.
    \end{equation}
    Moreover,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:smooth-spectral-linear-coeff-bound}
        d^{-1}\left\lVert \psi'(A)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\le d^{-1/2}\left\lVert \psi'\right\rVert_\infty.
    \end{equation}
    In particular, if $\|E\|_\mathrm{F} = \mathcal{O}(d^{-1/2})$, then
    \begin{equation}
        |R_{\psi,A}(E)| \leq C_{\psi,L} d^{-5/2}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-11 -->
<!-- lemma-12: body_md -->
\label{lem:spectral-taylor-4}
    Fix $0\le t\le T-1$ and recall from \eqref{eq:vectorized-AMP-recursion} that $G_t(v)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \operatorname{vec}(\sigma_t(\mathrm{mat}(v)))$.
    For each $y\in\mathbb R^p$, there exist a symmetric bilinear map
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbf{B}_{t,y}:(\mathbb R^p)^2 \to \mathbb R^p,
    \end{equation}
    a symmetric trilinear map
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbf{T}_{t,y}:(\mathbb R^p)^3 \to \mathbb R^p,
    \end{equation}
    and a remainder map
    \begin{equation}
        \rho_{t,y}^{(4)}:\mathbb R^p \to \mathbb R^p
    \end{equation}
    such that for every $g\in\mathbb R^p$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:local-spectral-third-order}
        G_t(y+g)-G_t(y)-DG_t(y)g
        =
        \mathbf{B}_{t,y}(g,g)+\mathbf{T}_{t,y}(g,g,g)+\rho_{t,y}^{(4)}(g).
    \end{equation}
    Moreover, with
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:M-sigma-4-def}
        M_{\sigma,4}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=
        \max_{0\,\le\, s\,\le\, T-1}\max_{1\,\le\, r\,\le\, 4}\left\lVert \sigma_s^{(r)}\right\rVert_\infty,
    \end{equation}
    there is a constant $C<\infty$, depending only on $M_{\sigma,4}$, such that
    for all $y\in\mathbb R^p$ and all $g,g_1,g_2,g_3,w\in\mathbb R^p$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:local-spectral-B-bound}
        \left\lVert \mathbf{B}_{t,y}(g_1,g_2)\right\rVert \le C \left\lVert g_1\right\rVert\left\lVert g_2\right\rVert,
    \end{equation}
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:local-spectral-T-bound}
        \left\lVert \mathbf{T}_{t,y}(g_1,g_2,g_3)\right\rVert \le C \left\lVert g_1\right\rVert\left\lVert g_2\right\rVert\left\lVert g_3\right\rVert,
    \end{equation}
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:local-spectral-rem-bound}
        \left\lVert \rho_{t,y}^{(4)}(g)\right\rVert \le C \left\lVert g\right\rVert^4,
    \end{equation}
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:local-spectral-B-HS}
        \big\|\left\langle w,\mathbf{B}_{t,y}(\cdot,\cdot)\right\rangle\big\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
        \le C \sqrt d \left\lVert w\right\rVert,
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:local-spectral-T-HS}
        \big\|\left\langle w,\mathbf{T}_{t,y}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\right\rangle\big\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
        \le C d \left\lVert w\right\rVert.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-12 -->
<!-- lemma-13: body_md -->
\label{lem:bilinear-matrix}
    Let $\mathfrak B:(\mathbb R^p)^2\to\mathbb R$ be bilinear. There exists a unique matrix $A\in\mathbb R^{p\times p}$ such that
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak B(x,y)=\left\langle x,Ay\right\rangle,
        \qquad x,y\in\mathbb R^p.
    \end{equation}
    If $M=(A+A^\top)/2$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak B(u,u)=\left\langle u,Mu\right\rangle,
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert M\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\le \left\lVert A\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}=\left\lVert \mathfrak B\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-13 -->
<!-- proof-33: body_md -->
The matrix $A$ is defined by $A_{ab}=\mathfrak B(e_a,e_b)$ in the standard basis. Since $u^\top Au=u^\top Mu$, the first identity is immediate. The Frobenius bound follows from orthogonal projection onto the symmetric subspace.
<!-- end proof-33 -->
<!-- lemma-14: body_md -->
\label{lem:trilinear-decomp}
    Let $\mathfrak T:(\mathbb R^p)^3 \to \mathbb R$ be trilinear and fix an orthonormal basis $(e_\nu)_{\nu = 1}^p$ of $\mathbb R^p$. Then there exist symmetric matrices $M_1, \ldots, M_p \in \mathbb R^{p \times p}$ and a vector $b \in \mathbb R^p$ such that for every $u \in \mathbb R^p$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:trilinear-decomp-main}
        \mathfrak T(u,u,u) = \left\langle b,u\right\rangle + \sum_{\nu = 1}^p \left\langle e_\nu,u\right\rangle \left(u^\top M_\nu u - \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr} M_\nu \right)
    \end{equation}
    Further, we have bounds
    \begin{equation}
        \sum_{\nu\,=\,1}^p \left\lVert M_\nu\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2 \,\le\, \left\lVert \mathfrak T\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}^2, \quad \left\lVert b\right\rVert \,\le\, p^{-1/2} \left\lVert \mathfrak T\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-14 -->
<!-- proof-34: body_md -->
For each $\nu \in [p]$, define bilinear form $\mathfrak B_\nu(x, y) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \mathfrak T(e_\nu, x, y)$. Let $A_\nu$ be the matrix representation of this bilinear form. From Lemma \ref{lem:bilinear-matrix} and let $M_\nu$ be its symmetric version. Then
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak T(u,u,u) \,=\, \sum_{\nu\,=\,1}^p \left\langle e_\nu,u\right\rangle \mathfrak B_\nu(u,u) \,=\, \sum_{\nu \,=\, 1}^p \left\langle e_\nu,u\right\rangle \left\langle u,M_\nu u\right\rangle
    \end{equation}
    Set $m_\nu \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr} M_\nu$ and $b \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \sum_{\nu = 1}^p m_\nu e_\nu$. This gives \eqref{eq:trilinear-decomp-main}. Furthermore, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \sum_{\nu\,=\,1}^p \left\lVert M_\nu\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2 \,\le\, \sum_{\nu\,=\,1}^p \left\lVert A_\nu\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2 \,=\, \sum_{\nu,a,b}^p \left\lvert \mathfrak T(e_\nu,e_a,e_b)\right\rvert^2 \,=\, \left\lVert \mathfrak T\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}^2
    \end{equation}
    Similarly,
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert b\right\rVert^2 \,=\, \sum_{\nu\,=\,1}^p m_\nu^2 \,\le\, \frac 1 p \sum_{\nu\,=\,1}^p \left\lVert M_\nu\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2 \,\le\, \frac 1 p \left\lVert \mathfrak T\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}^2
    \end{equation}
<!-- end proof-34 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem:hs-composition}
Let $\mathfrak B:(\mathbb R^p)^2\to\mathbb R$ be bilinear and let $A_1,A_2\in \mathbb R^{p \times p}$. Define
\begin{equation}
    \mathfrak B_{A_1,A_2}(u_1,u_2) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \mathfrak B(A_1u_1,A_2u_2).
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:hs-compose-bilinear}
    \left\lVert \mathfrak B_{A_1,A_2}\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}
    \le \left\lVert \mathfrak B\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS} \left\lVert A_1\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \left\lVert A_2\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}.
\end{equation}
If $\mathfrak T:(\mathbb R^p)^3\to\mathbb R$ is trilinear and $A_1,A_2,A_3:\mathbb R^p\to\mathbb R^p$ are linear, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:hs-compose-trilinear-direct}
    \left\lVert \mathfrak T(A_1\cdot,A_2\cdot,A_3\cdot)\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}
    \le \left\lVert \mathfrak T\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS} \left\lVert A_1\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \left\lVert A_2\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \left\lVert A_3\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}.
\end{equation}
Now let $\mathbf{B}:(\mathbb R^p)^2\to\mathbb R^p$ be bilinear and suppose that for some $K<\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:hs-compose-hyp}
    \left\lVert \left\langle w,\mathbf{B}(\cdot,\cdot)\right\rangle\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS} \le K \left\lVert w\right\rVert
    \qquad \text{for every } w\in\mathbb R^p.
\end{equation}
Let $A,U,V,W:\mathbb R^p\to\mathbb R^p$ be linear and define
\begin{equation}
    \mathfrak C(u_1,u_2,u_3)
    \mathrel{\mathop{:}}=
    \mathfrak B\big(Au_1, U\mathbf{B}(Vu_2,Wu_3)\big).
\end{equation}
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:hs-compose-trilinear}
    \left\lVert \mathfrak C\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}
    \le K \left\lVert \mathfrak B\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS} \left\lVert A\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \left\lVert U\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \left\lVert V\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} \left\lVert W\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-35: body_md -->
This is obtained from
    \cite{RubenssonEmanuelH2023Hodo}, Theorems 3.1 and 3.3 and the Hermitian
    formula in Section 4, applied to the path
    $X+\sum_{r=1}^m t_rH_r$. Since $\operatorname{vec}$ and $\mathrm{mat}$ are linear isometries,
    the same differentiability holds for $G$.

    It remains only to prove the dimension-free norm bound. Fix
    $\pi\in S_m$ and let $Z_\pi$ be the corresponding ordered summand in
    \eqref{eq:dk-formula}. Diagonalize $X=U\Lambda U^\top$ and set
    $\widetilde H_r\mathrel{\mathop{:}}= U^\top H_rU$. Then, for fixed endpoints $i_0,i_m$,
    \begin{equation}
        (U^\top Z_\pi U)_{i_0i_m}
        =
        \sum_{i_1,\dots,i_{m-1}=1}^d
        \varphi^{[m]}(\lambda_{i_0},\dots,\lambda_{i_m})
        (\widetilde H_{\pi(1)})_{i_0i_1}\cdots
        (\widetilde H_{\pi(m)})_{i_{m-1}i_m}.
    \end{equation}
    Therefore, with entrywise absolute values,
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lvert U^\top Z_\pi U\right\rvert_{i_0i_m}
        \le
        \left(\left\lVert \varphi^{[m]}\right\rVert_\infty
        \left\lvert \widetilde H_{\pi(1)}\right\rvert\cdots \left\lvert \widetilde H_{\pi(m)}\right\rvert\right)_{i_0, i_m}
    \end{equation}
    Frobenius submultiplicativity gives
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert Z_\pi\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}} \,\le\, \left\lVert \varphi^{[m]}\right\rVert_\infty \prod_{r\,=\,1}^m \left\lVert \widetilde H_r\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}} \,=\, \left\lVert \varphi^{[m]}\right\rVert_\infty \prod_{r\,=\,1}^m \left\lVert H_r\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}.
    \end{equation}
    Summing the $m!$ ordered terms and using the Hermite--Genocchi bound
    \eqref{eq:divided-difference-bound}, namely
    $\left\lVert \varphi^{[m]}\right\rVert_\infty\le \left\lVert \varphi^{(m)}\right\rVert_\infty/m!$, yields
    \eqref{eq:dk-frobenius-bound}. The vector bound
    \eqref{eq:dk-vector-bound} follows by applying the isometries $\operatorname{vec}$ and
    $\mathrm{mat}$.
<!-- end proof-35 -->
<!-- proof-36: body_md -->
Choose a smooth cutoff $\chi_L$ that equals one on $[-L,L]$ and is supported on $[-L-1,L+1]$, and set $\bar\psi=\chi_L\psi$. Its first three derivatives are bounded by a constant depending only on $L$ and on the corresponding local bounds for $\psi$. Moreover,
    \begin{equation*}
        A+sE=(1-s)A+s(A+E),
        \qquad 0\leq s\leq1,
    \end{equation*}
    so \eqref{eq:smooth-spectral-local-domain} and convexity of the operator norm imply $\|A+sE\|_\mathrm{op}\leq L$. Hence $\psi$ and $\bar\psi$ induce the same spectral functional along the entire segment from $A$ to $A+E$. It is therefore enough to prove the claim for the bounded $C^3$ function $\bar\psi$; to simplify notation, we again denote it by $\psi$.

    Lemma \ref{lem:dk-bounds} implies that the matrix map $A\mapsto \psi(A)$ is $C^3$; hence the normalized trace functional $\Phi_\psi$ is $C^3$ on $\textsf{Sym}_d$.

    We first identify the linear term. If
    $A=\sum_{a=1}^d \lambda_a P_a$ is a spectral decomposition of
    $A$, then the first-order Daleckii--Krein formula gives
    \begin{equation}
        D(\psi(\cdot))(A)[E]
        =
        \sum_{a,b=1}^d \psi^{[1]}(\lambda_a,\lambda_b)P_a E P_b.
    \end{equation}
    Taking traces and using cyclic invariance leaves only the terms $a=b$, and
    $\psi^{[1]}(\lambda_a,\lambda_a)=\psi'(\lambda_a)$. Therefore
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:Phi-linear-term}
        D\Phi_\psi(A)[E]
        =\frac1d\mathrm{Tr}\big(\psi'(A)E\big).
    \end{equation}

    Define the symmetric bilinear form
    \begin{equation}
        B_{\psi,A}(H_1,H_2)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \frac12 D^2\Phi_\psi(A)[H_1,H_2].
    \end{equation}
    Differentiating \eqref{eq:Phi-linear-term} in the direction $H_1$ and
    testing against $H_2$ gives
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:Phi-Hessian-trace}
        D^2\Phi_\psi(A)[H_1,H_2]
        =\frac1d\mathrm{Tr}\big(D(\psi') (A)[H_1]H_2\big).
    \end{equation}
    Hence, by Cauchy--Schwarz and Lemma \ref{lem:dk-bounds} applied to
    $\psi'$ with $m=1$,
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lvert D^2\Phi_\psi(A)[H_1,H_2]\right\rvert
        \le C_\psi d^{-1}\left\lVert H_1\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\left\lVert H_2\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}.
    \end{equation}
    The Riesz representation theorem on the inner product space $(\textsf{Sym}_d,\langle\cdot,
    \cdot\rangle_\mathrm{F})$ therefore gives a unique self-adjoint linear operator
    $\mathcal K_{\psi,A}:\textsf{Sym}_d\to\textsf{Sym}_d$ such that
    \begin{equation}
        B_{\psi,A}(H_1,H_2)=\left\langle H_1,\mathcal K_{\psi,A}(H_2)\right\rangle,
        \qquad H_1,H_2\in\textsf{Sym}_d,
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \mathcal K_{\psi,A}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}\to\mathrm{F}}\le C_\psi d^{-1}.
    \end{equation}
    Thus
    \begin{equation}
        Q_{\psi,A}(E)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}= B_{\psi,A}(E,E)
        =\left\langle E,\mathcal K_{\psi,A}(E)\right\rangle
        =\frac12D^2\Phi_\psi(A)[E,E].
    \end{equation}

    Taylor's theorem with integral remainder gives
    \begin{equation}
        \Phi_\psi(A+E)-\Phi_\psi(A)-D\Phi_\psi(A)[E]-\frac12D^2\Phi_\psi(A)[E,E]
        =
        \int_0^1 \frac{(1-s)^2}{2}D^3\Phi_\psi(A+sE)[E,E,E]ds.
    \end{equation}
    We define the right-hand side to be $R_{\psi,A}(E)$. Differentiating
    \eqref{eq:Phi-Hessian-trace} once more, and using symmetry of higher
    derivatives, yields
    \begin{equation}
        D^3\Phi_\psi(B)[H_1,H_2,H_3]
        =
        \frac1d\mathrm{Tr}\big(D^2(\psi')(B)[H_1,H_2]H_3\big).
    \end{equation}
    Applying Lemma \ref{lem:dk-bounds} to $\psi'$ with $m=2$ and then
    Cauchy--Schwarz gives
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lvert D^3\Phi_\psi(B)[H_1,H_2,H_3]\right\rvert
        \le C_\psi d^{-1}
        \left\lVert H_1\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\left\lVert H_2\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}\left\lVert H_3\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}.
    \end{equation}
    Consequently
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lvert R_{\psi,A}(E)\right\rvert\le C_\psi d^{-1}\left\lVert E\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^3,
    \end{equation}
    which is \eqref{eq:smooth-spectral-remainder-bound}. This proves
    \eqref{eq:smooth-spectral-taylor}.

    For the vector form, let
    \begin{equation}
        K_{\psi,A}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \operatorname{vec}\circ \mathcal K_{\psi,A}\circ \mathrm{mat},
    \end{equation}
    viewed as its matrix in the standard Euclidean coordinates of $\mathbb R^p$.
    Since $\operatorname{vec}$ is an isometry, $K_{\psi,A}$ is symmetric and has the same
    operator norm as $\mathcal K_{\psi,A}$. Finally,
    \begin{equation}
        d^{-1}\left\lVert \psi'(A)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}
        \le d^{-1}\sqrt d\,\left\lVert \psi'\right\rVert_\infty
        =d^{-1/2}\left\lVert \psi'\right\rVert_\infty,
    \end{equation}
    and the final claim follows immediately from
    \eqref{eq:smooth-spectral-remainder-bound}.
<!-- end proof-36 -->
<!-- proof-37: body_md -->
Fix $t$, write $\varphi=\sigma_t$ and $G=G_t$. Since
    $\varphi\in C_b^4(\mathbb R)$ and $\left\lVert \varphi^{(r)}\right\rVert_\infty\le M_{\sigma,4}$ for
    $1\le r\le4$, Lemma \ref{lem:dk-bounds} implies that $G$ is $C^4$ and
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:G-derivative-bounds-up-to-4}
        \sup_{z\in\mathbb R^p}\left\lVert D^rG(z)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\le M_{\sigma,4},
        \qquad 1\le r\le 4.
    \end{equation}

    Define
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbf{B}_{t,y}(g_1,g_2) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \frac12 D^2G(y)[g_1,g_2],
    \end{equation}
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbf{T}_{t,y}(g_1,g_2,g_3) \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \frac16 D^3G(y)[g_1,g_2,g_3],
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        \rho_{t,y}^{(4)}(g)
        \mathrel{\mathop{:}}=
        \int_0^1 \frac{(1-s)^3}{6} D^4G(y+sg)[g,g,g,g] ds.
    \end{equation}
    Taylor's theorem with integral remainder gives
    \eqref{eq:local-spectral-third-order}.  The pointwise bounds \eqref{eq:local-spectral-B-bound}--\eqref{eq:local-spectral-rem-bound} follow immediately from \eqref{eq:G-derivative-bounds-up-to-4}.

    It remains to prove the Hilbert--Schmidt bounds. Fix $w\in\mathbb R^p$ and write $W=\mathrm{mat}(w)$. Let
    \begin{equation}
        Y \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \mathrm{mat}(y) \,=\, \sum_{a\,=\,1}^d \lambda_a P_a,
        \qquad P_a = u_a u_a^\top,
    \end{equation}
    with eigenvalues repeated according to multiplicity. For matrices
    $H_j=\mathrm{mat}(g_j)$, the second-order Daleckii--Krein formula in Lemma \ref{lem:dk-bounds} gives
    \begin{equation}
        \left\langle w,D^2G(y)[g_1,g_2]\right\rangle
        =
        \sum_{\pi\in S_2}
        \sum_{a,b,c=1}^d
        \varphi^{[2]}(\lambda_a,\lambda_b,\lambda_c)
        \mathrm{Tr}(WP_a H_{\pi(1)} P_b H_{\pi(2)} P_c).
    \end{equation}
    Define the ordered bilinear form on the full matrix space $\mathbb R^{d\times d}$ by
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak B_{y,w}(H_1,H_2)
        \mathrel{\mathop{:}}=
        \sum_{a,b,c=1}^d
        \varphi^{[2]}(\lambda_a,\lambda_b,\lambda_c)
        \mathrm{Tr}(WP_a H_1 P_b H_2 P_c).
    \end{equation}
    Since $\mathbf{B}_{t,y}=\frac12D^2G(y)$,
    \begin{equation}
        \left\langle w,\mathbf{B}_{t,y}(g_1,g_2)\right\rangle
        =\frac12\Big(\mathfrak B_{y,w}(H_1,H_2)+\mathfrak B_{y,w}(H_2,H_1)\Big).
    \end{equation}
    Permuting arguments preserves Hilbert--Schmidt norm, and restricting a
    multilinear form from $\mathbb R^{d\times d}$ to the subspace $\textsf{Sym}_d$ cannot
    increase its Hilbert--Schmidt norm. Thus
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:B-scalar-HS-reduce}
        \big\|\left\langle w,\mathbf{B}_{t,y}(\cdot,\cdot)\right\rangle\big\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
        \le \left\lVert \mathfrak B_{y,w}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}(\mathbb R^{d\times d})}.
    \end{equation}

    Let
    \begin{equation}
        \mathsf{E}_{ab}\mathrel{\mathop{:}}= u_a u_b^\top,
        \qquad 1\le a,b\le d.
    \end{equation}
    Then $(\mathsf{E}_{ab})_{a,b}$ is a Frobenius-orthonormal basis of
    $\mathbb R^{d\times d}$. A direct computation shows that
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak B_{y,w}(\mathsf{E}_{ab},\mathsf{E}_{bc})
        =
        \varphi^{[2]}(\lambda_a,\lambda_b,\lambda_c)(u_c^\top W u_a),
    \end{equation}
    while $\mathfrak B_{y,w}(\mathsf{E}_{rs},\mathsf{E}_{tu})=0$ unless $s=t$. Hence
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \mathfrak B_{y,w}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^2
        =
        \sum_{a,b,c=1}^d
        \left\lvert \varphi^{[2]}(\lambda_a,\lambda_b,\lambda_c)\right\rvert^2
        \left\lvert u_c^\top W u_a\right\rvert^2
        \le C d\left\lVert W\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^2
        = C d\left\lVert w\right\rVert^2.
    \end{equation}
    Combining this with \eqref{eq:B-scalar-HS-reduce} proves
    \eqref{eq:local-spectral-B-HS}.

    The trilinear estimate is analogous. Define the trilinear form on
    $\mathbb R^{d\times d}$ by
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak T_{y,w}(H_1,H_2,H_3)
        \mathrel{\mathop{:}}=
        \sum_{a,b,c,e=1}^d
        \varphi^{[3]}(\lambda_a,\lambda_b,\lambda_c,\lambda_e)
        \mathrm{Tr}(WP_a H_1 P_b H_2 P_c H_3 P_e).
    \end{equation}
    Since $\mathbf{T}_{t,y}=\frac16D^3G(y)$,
    $\left\langle w,\mathbf{T}_{t,y}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\right\rangle$ is the average over the six
    permutations of the arguments of $\mathfrak T_{y,w}$, restricted to $\textsf{Sym}_d$. Hence
    \begin{equation}
        \big\|\left\langle w,\mathbf{T}_{t,y}(\cdot,\cdot,\cdot)\right\rangle\big\|_{\mathrm{HS}}
        \le \left\lVert \mathfrak T_{y,w}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}(\mathbb R^{d\times d})}.
    \end{equation}
    Moreover,
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak T_{y,w}(\mathsf{E}_{ab},\mathsf{E}_{bc},\mathsf{E}_{ce})
        =
        \varphi^{[3]}(\lambda_a,\lambda_b,\lambda_c,\lambda_e)(u_e^\top W u_a),
    \end{equation}
    and the form vanishes on all other triples of matrix units. Therefore
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \mathfrak T_{y,w}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^2
        \le C d^2\left\lVert W\right\rVert_{\mathrm{F}}^2
        = C d^2\left\lVert w\right\rVert^2.
    \end{equation}
    This proves \eqref{eq:local-spectral-T-HS}.
<!-- end proof-37 -->
<!-- proof-38: body_md -->
Let $L$ be the matrix representation of the scalar bilinear form $\mathfrak B$, so
    $\mathfrak B(x,y)=\left\langle x,Ly\right\rangle$ and $\left\lVert \mathfrak B\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}=\left\lVert L\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}$. Then
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak B_{A_1,A_2}(u_1,u_2)=\left\langle u_1,A_1^\top L A_2 u_2\right\rangle,
    \end{equation}
    and therefore
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \mathfrak B_{A_1,A_2}\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}
        =\left\lVert A_1^\top L A_2\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}
        \le \left\lVert L\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}\left\lVert A_1\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}\left\lVert A_2\right\rVert_\mathrm{op},
    \end{equation}
    proving \eqref{eq:hs-compose-bilinear}.

    For \eqref{eq:hs-compose-trilinear-direct}, identify $\mathfrak T$ with its
    coefficient tensor $T=(T_{abc})$ in the standard orthonormal basis. The
    coefficient tensor of $\mathfrak T(A_1\cdot,A_2\cdot,A_3\cdot)$ is
    $(A_1^\top\otimes A_2^\top\otimes A_3^\top)T$ after vectorization. Hence
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \mathfrak T(A_1\cdot,A_2\cdot,A_3\cdot)\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}
        \le
        \left\lVert A_1^\top\otimes A_2^\top\otimes A_3^\top\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}\left\lVert \mathfrak T\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}
        =
        \left\lVert A_1\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}\left\lVert A_2\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}\left\lVert A_3\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}\left\lVert \mathfrak T\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}.
    \end{equation}

    We now prove \eqref{eq:hs-compose-trilinear}. Let $L$ again represent
    $\mathfrak B$. For each standard basis vector $e_a$, define the scalar bilinear form
    \begin{equation}
        G_a(u_2,u_3)\mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \left\langle U^\top L^\top A e_a,\mathbf{B}(u_2,u_3)\right\rangle.
    \end{equation}
    Then
    \begin{equation}
        \mathfrak C(e_a,u_2,u_3)=G_a(Vu_2,Wu_3).
    \end{equation}
    By \eqref{eq:hs-compose-hyp},
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert G_a\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}
        \le K\left\lVert U^\top L^\top A e_a\right\rVert
        \le K\left\lVert U\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}\left\lVert L^\top A e_a\right\rVert.
    \end{equation}
    Applying the already proved bilinear composition bound to $G_a$ gives
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \mathfrak C(e_a,\cdot,\cdot)\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}
        \le
        K\left\lVert U\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}\left\lVert V\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}\left\lVert W\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}\left\lVert L^\top A e_a\right\rVert.
    \end{equation}
    Summing over $a$ yields
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{aligned}
        \left\lVert \mathfrak C\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}^2
        &=\sum_{a=1}^p\left\lVert \mathfrak C(e_a,\cdot,\cdot)\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}^2 \\
        &\le K^2\left\lVert U\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2\left\lVert V\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2\left\lVert W\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2
        \sum_{a=1}^p\left\lVert L^\top A e_a\right\rVert^2 \\
        &=K^2\left\lVert U\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2\left\lVert V\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2\left\lVert W\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2\left\lVert L^\top A\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2 \\
        &\le K^2\left\lVert \mathfrak B\right\rVert_\mathrm{HS}^2\left\lVert A\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2\left\lVert U\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2\left\lVert V\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2\left\lVert W\right\rVert_\mathrm{op}^2.
        \end{aligned}
    \end{equation}
    Taking square roots proves \eqref{eq:hs-compose-trilinear}.
<!-- end proof-38 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:adamczak-opnorm}
    Let $X_1, \ldots, X_N \in \mathbb R^p$ be independent centered isotropic random vectors. Assume that for some constants $\psi, K, c > 0$, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \sup_{j \,\le\, N} \sup_{y \,\in\, S^{p-1}} \left\lVert \left\langle X_j,y\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_1} \,\le\, \psi
    \end{equation}
    and
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb P\left(\max_{j\,\le\, N}\frac{\|X_j\|}{\sqrt p}\,>\, K\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{N}{p}\right)^{1/4}\right\}\right)\,\le\, e^{-c\sqrt p}.
    \end{equation}
    Let $A = [X_1, \ldots, X_N] \in \mathbb R^{p \times N}$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb P(\left\lVert A\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} > C \sqrt{p}) \le 2 e^{-c'\sqrt{p}}
    \end{equation}
    for some constants $C,c'>0$ depending only on $\psi,K$ and an upper bound for $N/p$.
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-39: body_md -->
This is Theorem 2 of \cite{1012.0294}.
<!-- end proof-39 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:sensing-envelope}
    For any fixed $m \ge 1$, there exists $C_m < \infty$ such that uniformly in $i$,
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert K_i\right\rVert_{L^m(\mathrm{op})} \leq C_m, \quad \left\lVert F_{\setminus i} \epsilon\right\rVert_{L^m} \leq C_m d
    \end{equation}
    Moreover, for $u \in \{f_i, \widetilde f_i\}$,
    \begin{equation}
        \|u\|_{L^m} \leq C_m.
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:one-column-moments}
    Let $u\in\{f_i,\widetilde f_i\}$. Let $a,b\in\mathbb R^p$ and $A\in\mathbb R^{p\times p}$ be $\mathcal G_i$-measurable, and let $A_{\mathrm s}=(A+A^\top)/2$.
    Then almost surely,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:linear-moment-1}
        \mathbb{E}[\left\langle u,a\right\rangle\mid \mathcal G_i]=0
    \end{equation}
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:linear-moment-2}
        \mathbb{E}[\left\langle u,a\right\rangle\left\langle u,b\right\rangle\mid \mathcal G_i]=\frac1p\left\langle a,b\right\rangle
    \end{equation}
    Moreover, for every integer $m\ge 1$ there exists $C_m<\infty$ such that almost surely,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:linear-moment-lm}
        \mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert \left\langle u,a\right\rangle\right\rvert^m\mid \mathcal G_i\right]\le C_m p^{-m/2}\left\lVert a\right\rVert^m
    \end{equation}
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:quadratic-moment-lm}
        \mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert \left\langle u,Au\right\rangle-\frac1p\mathrm{Tr} A\right\rvert^m\mid \mathcal G_i\right]
        \le C_m d^{-m/2}\left\lVert A_{\mathrm s}\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}^m
    \end{equation}
    Consequently, after averaging over $\mathcal G_i$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:linear-moment-uncond}
        \left\lVert \left\langle u,a\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^m}\le C_m d^{-1}\left\lVert a\right\rVert_{L^m}
    \end{equation}
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:quadratic-moment-uncond}
        \left\|\left\langle u,Au\right\rangle-\frac1p\mathrm{Tr} A\right\|_{L^m}
        \le C_m d^{-1/2}\left\lVert A_{\mathrm s}\right\rVert_{L^{2m}(\mathrm{op})}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- corollary-8: body_md -->
\label{cor:one-column-moments}
For every integer $m\ge 1$ there exists $C_m<\infty$ such that the following hold uniformly in $i$ and in $u\in\{f_i,\widetilde f_i\}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
    \item If $a$ is $\mathcal G_i$-measurable, then
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \left\langle u,a\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^m}\le C_m d^{-1}\left\lVert a\right\rVert_{L^m}.
    \end{equation}
    In particular, if $\left\lVert a\right\rVert_{L^m}\le C d^{-1/2}$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert \left\langle u,a\right\rangle\right\rVert_{L^m}\le C_m d^{-3/2}.
    \end{equation}
    \item If $A$ is $\mathcal G_i$-measurable and $A_{\mathrm s}=(A+A^\top)/2$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \left\|\left\langle u,Au\right\rangle-\frac1p\mathrm{Tr} A\right\|_{L^m}\le C_m d^{-1/2}\left\lVert A_{\mathrm s}\right\rVert_{L^{2m}(\mathrm{op})}.
    \end{equation}
    In particular, if $\left\lVert A_{\mathrm s}\right\rVert_{L^{2m}(\mathrm{op})}\le C$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \left\|\left\langle u,Au\right\rangle-\frac1p\mathrm{Tr} A\right\|_{L^m}\le C_m d^{-1/2},
    \end{equation}
    while if $\left\lVert A_{\mathrm s}\right\rVert_{L^{2m}(\mathrm{op})}\le C d^{-1}$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \left\|\left\langle u,Au\right\rangle-\frac1p\mathrm{Tr} A\right\|_{L^m}\le C_m d^{-3/2}.
    \end{equation}
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end corollary-8 -->
<!-- proof-40: body_md -->
This follows directly from Lemma~\ref{lem:one-column-moments}.
<!-- end proof-40 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem:refined-centered-quad}
    For every integer $m \ge 2$ there exists $C_m < \infty$ such that the following holds uniformly in $i$ and in $u \in \{f_i, \widetilde f_i\}$. If $A \in \mathbb R^{p \times p}$ is $\mathcal{G}_i$-measurable and $A_s = \frac{A + A^\top}{2}$, then we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:refined-centered-quad-bound}
        \left\lVert u^\top A u - \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr} A\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_m d^{-3/2} \left\lVert A_s\right\rVert_{L^{2m}(\mathrm{F})}
    \end{equation}
    In particular, if $\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{L^{2m}(\mathrm{F})} \le C d^{-1/2}$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert  u^\top A u - \frac 1 p \mathrm{Tr} A\right\rVert_{L^m} \le C_m d^{-2}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{lem:gaussian-quad-cumulants}
    Let $x \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I_d)$. Let $M \in \textsf{Sym}_d$ and define
    \begin{equation}
        q_M(x) = x^\top M x - \mathrm{Tr}(M)
    \end{equation}
    For random variables $X_1, \ldots, X_r$ define their joint cumulant by
    \begin{equation}
        \kappa(Z_1, \ldots, Z_r) \,\mathrel{\mathop{:}}=\, \partial_{t_1} \cdots \partial_{t_r} \log \mathbb{E} \exp\left( \sum_{j \,=\, 1}^t t_j X_j  \right)\Bigg|_{t \,=\, 0}
    \end{equation}
    Then for every integer $ r \ge 2$ and every $M_1, \cdots, M_r \in \textsf{Sym}_d$, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \kappa\left(q_{M_1}(x), \ldots, q_{M_r}(x) \right) \,=\, 2^{r-1} \sum_{\sigma \,\in\, \mathfrak C_r} \mathrm{Tr}\left( M_{\sigma(1)} \cdots M_{\sigma(r)} \right)
    \end{equation}
    where $\mathfrak C_r$ is $S_r$ quotient by the equivalence of differing by a cycle. In particular, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \kappa(q_M(x), q_N(x)) = 2 \mathrm{Tr}(MN)
    \end{equation}
    \begin{equation}
        \kappa\left( q_{M_1}(x), q_{M_2}(x), q_{M_3}(x), q_{M_4}(x) \right) \,=\, 8 \sum_{\sigma \,\in\, \mathfrak C_4} \mathrm{Tr}(M_{\sigma(1)}M_{\sigma(2)} M_{\sigma(3)} M_{\sigma(4)})
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- proof-41: body_md -->
We first prove the $m$th-moment bound for the sensing vector norm. If $u = \widetilde f_i \sim \mathcal{N}(0, I_p/p)$, then we have $p\|u\|^2 \sim \chi_p^2$. Therefore, $\|u\|_{L^m} \leq C_m$ for every $m \geq 1$. If $u = f_i$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \|u\|^2 \,=\, \frac{1}{d(d + 1)} \left\lVert x_ix_i^\top - I_d\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2 \,=\, \frac{\left\lVert x_i\right\rVert^4 - 2 \left\lVert x_i\right\rVert^2 + d}{d(d + 1)}.
    \end{equation}
    Observe that $\left\lVert x_i\right\rVert^2 \sim \chi_d^2$. Therefore, the RHS has every moment bounded uniformly in $d$. So $\|u\|_{L^m} \leq C_m$. Next, denote the non-leave-one-out columns by
    \begin{equation}
        v_j^{(i)} \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \begin{cases}
            \widetilde f_j, & 1 \leq j < i \\
            f_j, & i < j \leq n
        \end{cases}
    \end{equation}
    and let $V_i \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= [v_j^{(i)}]_{j \neq i} \in \mathbb R^{p \times (n - 1)}$. By the definition of $K_i$ in \eqref{eq:Ki-def}, we write $K_i = V_iV_i^\top$. Set $X_j^{(i)} = \sqrt{p}\, v_j^{(i)}$. For the Gaussian columns, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \sup_{y \,\in\, S^{p-1}} \left\lVert \left\langle  X_j^{(i)},y\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_1} \,\leq\, \|\mathcal{N}(0,1)\|_{\psi_1} \,\leq\, C
    \end{equation}
    where $\left\lVert \cdot\right\rVert_{\psi_1}$ denotes the sub-exponential norm. In particular, $\frac{\left\lVert X_j^{(i)}\right\rVert}{\sqrt{p}}$ has sub-exponential tails with constants independent of $d$. For the RO columns, if $y = \operatorname{vec}(M)$ with $\left\lVert M\right\rVert_\mathrm{F} = 1$, then
    \begin{equation}
        \left\langle X_j^{(i)},y\right\rangle = \frac{x_j^\top M x_j - \mathrm{Tr} M}{\sqrt{2}}.
    \end{equation}
    Writing $M = U \Lambda U^\top$, this is a linear combination of the centered variables $(\xi_r^2 - 1)$ with coefficients the eigenvalues of $M$. Since $\sum_r \lambda_r^2 = \left\lVert M\right\rVert_\mathrm{F}^2 = 1$, standard bounds for Gaussian quadratic forms (Hanson--Wright) give
    \begin{equation}
        \sup_{y \in S^{p-1}} \left\lVert \left\langle X_j^{(i)},y\right\rangle\right\rVert_{\psi_1} \leq C.
    \end{equation}
    Moreover,
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{\left\lVert X_j^{(i)}\right\rVert^2}{p} = \left\lVert v_j^{(i)}\right\rVert^2,
    \end{equation}
    and for the rank-one columns this equals
    \begin{equation}
        \frac{\left\lVert x_j\right\rVert^4 - 2 \left\lVert x_j\right\rVert^2 + d}{d(d + 1)}.
    \end{equation}
    So for the Gaussian columns, we have from sub-gaussianity
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb P\left(\left\lVert X^{(i)}_j\right\rVert \,>\, 2\sqrt{p} \right) \,\leq\, 2e^{-cp}, \quad j \,<\, i,
    \end{equation}
    and for the rank-one columns, we have from the expression above and a $\chi_d^2$ tail bound (which is sub-exponential)
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb P\left(\left\lVert X^{(i)}_j\right\rVert \,>\, 2\sqrt{p} \right) \,\leq\, 2 e^{-c\sqrt{p}}, \quad j \,>\, i.
    \end{equation}
    Since $n\leq C_0p$, a union bound over the $n$ columns shows that the boundedness condition from \cite[Eq. 3]{1012.0294} holds uniformly in $i$ for $V_i$ with a deterministic constant $K$,
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:boundedness-event}
        \mathbb P \left(
        \max_{j\ne i}\frac{\left\lVert X_j^{(i)}\right\rVert}{\sqrt p} > K\max\left\{1,\left(\frac{n-1}{p}\right)^{1/4}\right\}
    \right) \leq e^{-c\sqrt{p}}.
    \end{equation}
    Therefore, the independent columns of $V_i$ satisfy the conditions of Lemma \ref{lem:adamczak-opnorm} \cite[Thm. 2]{1012.0294} with constants independent of $i$.
    With $A_i \mathrel{\mathop{:}}= \sqrt{p} V_i$, we have
    \begin{equation}\label{eq:operator-bound-prob}
        \mathbb P\left(\|A_i\|_\mathrm{op} > C \sqrt{\max\{p, n\}} + 6 \max_{j\neq i}\left\lVert X_j^{(i)}\right\rVert \right) \leq e^{-c\sqrt{p}}.
    \end{equation}
    From \eqref{eq:boundedness-event} and \eqref{eq:operator-bound-prob}, we have
    \begin{equation}
        \mathbb P\left(\left\lVert A_i\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} > C \sqrt{p} \right) \leq 2 e^{-c\sqrt{p}}.
    \end{equation}
    This is equivalent to $\mathbb P(\left\lVert K_i\right\rVert_\mathrm{op} > C) \leq 2 e^{-c\sqrt{p}}$.
    To obtain moment bounds from this probability estimate, we also control $\|K_i\|_\mathrm{op}$ on the exceptional event. Since $K_i$ is positive semidefinite,
    \begin{equation}
        \left\lVert K_i\right\rVert_{\mathrm{op}}\le \mathrm{Tr}(K_i)
        =\,\sum_{j\,<\,i}\left\lVert \widetilde f_j\right\rVert^2+\sum_{j\,>\,i}\left\lVert f_j\right\rVert^2.
    \end{equation}
    By the one-column bounds proved above and Minkowski,
    \begin{equation}
        \big\|\mathrm{Tr}(K_i)\big\|_{L^{2m}}
        \le \sum_{j<i}\left\lVert \widetilde f_j\right\rVert^2_{L^{2m}}
